Please also feel free to open a pull request to add it! Before requesting a package or new version, please try installing it on an OT-2 first; only packages with native code components need to be here, since pure-python packages are installable from PyPI.\\n\\nBe aware that the OT-2 system can't always handle the addition of a specific package. For instance, the package might be a thin wrapper around a system library that is not available on the OT-2; or it might have a toolchain that does not allow cross-compilation with the tools we have available.\\n\\n...\\n```File: `README.md`\\n```markdown\\n# opentrons-python-packages\\nA repository hosting prebuilds of Python packages suitable for installing on an Opentrons robot.\\n\\n**This repository is under construction and the code here has not yet been released.**\\n\\nThe OT-2 uses a custom Buildroot-created Linux system running on a Raspberry Pi. Because it is a custom system, it cannot use binary python packages made with Raspbian in mind; because it is an arm7hf architecture, it cannot use more general manylinux wheels made with x86-64 or arm64 in mind. Since the OT-2 itself does not have a compiler, we use this repository to provide prebuilt python wheels that are installable on an OT-2 by using a [PEP-503](https://peps.python.org/pep-0503) simple package index.\\n\\nAny package identified here can be installed on an OT-2 through `pip`.\\n\\n## Requesting Packages\\n...\\n## Building, and Dependencies To Build\\n...\\n## Build Tools\\n...\\n## Adding a New Package\\n...\\n## How does this all work, anyway?\\n...\\n```File: `README.md`\\n```markdown\\n# opentrons-python-packages\\n...\\n## Building, and Dependencies To Build\\n\\nThis repo is intended to work in production on github CI, but you can test builds locally (and should, since some packages can have surprising build system requirements). The entry point for building is `./build-packages`. It's a python script that should work on anything above python 3.7. You can get usage by running `./build-packages --help`.\\n\\nYou'll also need to have Docker installed and running and accessible with how you call `./build-packages` (i.e., if you don't want to use `sudo` then the docker socket should be user-accessible).\\n\\n### Machine Compatibility for Building\\n...\\n```File: `README.md`\\n```markdown\\n# opentrons-python-packages\\n...\\n## Building, and Dependencies To Build\\n...\\n### Machine Compatibility for Building\\n\\nThis is tested and maintained for x64 docker. It should work if you run it from\\n- An x86_64 linux host\\n  - Including WSL\\n- An x86_64 OSX host\\n\\nIt might work on a native Windows x86_64 host.\\n\\nIt definitely will not work on any machine architecture that presents the docker container with an arm64 environment (e.g. M1/M2 macs, phones, embedded devices). The buildroot SDK that powers the package builds needs to run in an x86_64 architecture. If that's all you have, you'll have to run it in a cross-architecture vm.\\n\\n...\\n```File: `README.md`\\n```markdown\\n# opentrons-python-packages\\n...\\n## How does this all work, anyway?\\n\\nSee the README.md in `tools/`!\\n\\n```\",\"languageName\":\"Markdown\",\"path\":\"README.md\",\"range\":{\"start\":2,\"end\":55},\"ref\":\"refs/heads/main\",\"type\":\"snippet\",\"url\":\"https://github.com/sgbaird/opentrons-python-packages/blob/7a3347c293325ac7dc85e6b117e36dfc03be0d7a/README.md#L2-L55\"},{\"contents\":\"File: `tools/README.md`\\n````markdown\\n# Build Tools\\n...\\n## How does this all work, anyway?\\n\\nThe core of the problem this package solves is building python packages for arm7hf on an x86_64 machine - cross compiling them. To do this it takes a lot of cues and support from buildroot.\\n\\nWe need a stable environment for the compilation, and we'll be messing with shell settings, so we do the work in docker. That means we have code that has to run in docker (container side) and code that has to run outside of docker, to manage docker (host-side).\\n\\n### Host side\\n...\\n### Container side\\n...\\n````File: `tools/README.md`\\n````markdown\\n# Build Tools\\n\\nThis subdirectory holds the local build tools we use for building the python packages. They are responsible for build orchestration and kicking off everything.\\n\\nThis tooling uses [poetry](python-poetry.org) with some plugins:\\n- [poetry-dynamic-versioning](https://pypi.org/project/poetry-dynamic-versioning/). to take versions from git\\n- [poe-the-poet](https://pypi.org/project/poethepoet/) to run tasks like test\\n\\nYou don't need to install these to _use_ the tooling - docker takes care of that - but you do need to install them to _change_ the tooling.\\n\\nYou do need to install docker for your preferred platform.\\n\\n## Setting up to develop the build tools\\n...\\n## Using Poetry\\n...\\n## Testing Build Tools\\n...\\n## Testing Content\\n...\\n## How does this all work, anyway?\\n...\\n````File: `tools/README.md`\\n````markdown\\n# Build Tools\\n...\\n## How does this all work, anyway?\\n...\\n### Container side\\n\\nThe container side code has to actually build all the packages. This duplicates some of the functionality of buildroot. Its job is to\\n1. Find the package and run its `build.py` (in `builder/package_build/orchestrate.py`)\\n2. Make sure we have everything we need to build the package, such as \\n   - package sources (`builder/package_build/download.py`)\\n   - package build dependencies (`builder/package_build/build_wheel.py`)\\n   - an activated buildroot sdk\\n   - an activated python virtual environment with the build dependencies\\n   - correct environments for forcing python to cross-compile\\n3. Actually run the build and harvest the results (also `build_wheel.py`)\\n\\nThe most complex part of this is making sure there's a correct environment to build things. The environment is important because it's the only way to pass certain options to the wheel builder (like the platform it should compile for) and provide cross compilation tools. This is done by having a long-running interactive shell that we communicate with in `builder/package_build/shell_environment.py`. \\n\\n````File: `tools/README.md`\\n````markdown\\n# Build Tools\\n...\\n## How does this all work, anyway?\\n...\\n### Host side\\n\\nThe host-side code is in `builder/host` and `builder/common`. The job of this code is to build or pull docker containers, and then run a docker container with correct flags. That's not a lot, and that's good, because that means we can have approximately 0 dependencies locally. This is why you can run `build-packages` with just python without having to do poetry setup.\\n\\n...\\n````\",\"languageName\":\"Markdown\",\"path\":\"tools/README.md\",\"range\":{\"start\":3,\"end\":76},\"ref\":\"refs/heads/main\",\"type\":\"snippet\",\"url\":\"https://github.com/sgbaird/opentrons-python-packages/blob/7a3347c293325ac7dc85e6b117e36dfc03be0d7a/tools/README.md#L3-L76\"},{\"contents\":\"File: The job of this code is to build or pull docker containers, and then run a docker container with correct flags. That's not a lot, and that's good, because that means we can have approximately 0 dependencies locally. 2025-06-13T00:38:42.1601159Z     107.    with tarfile.open(archive, "r") as tf:

2025-06-13T00:38:42.1602359Z     109.        unpacked = [

2025-06-13T00:38:42.1607436Z     121.    path: Path, archive: Path, from_archive_path: Path, *, context: GlobalBuildContext
2025-06-13T00:38:42.1608052Z     122.) -> Path:
2025-06-13T00:38:42.1608626Z     123.    context.write(f"Unzipping {archive} to {path}")
2025-06-13T00:38:42.1609138Z     124.    unzipped: list[Path] = []
2025-06-13T00:38:42.1609583Z     125.    with zipfile.ZipFile(archive) as zf:
2025-06-13T00:38:42.1610050Z     126.        for member in zf.infolist():
2025-06-13T00:38:42.1610558Z     127.            # skip archive members not in the from_archive_path
2025-06-13T00:38:42.1611041Z     128.            try:
2025-06-13T00:38:42.1611577Z     129.                common = Path(os.path.commonpath([member.filename, from_archive_path]))
2025-06-13T00:38:42.1612195Z     130.            except ValueError:
2025-06-13T00:38:42.1612572Z     131.                pass
2025-06-13T00:38:42.1612959Z     132.            if common != from_archive_path:
2025-06-13T00:38:42.1613389Z     133.                continue
2025-06-13T00:38:42.1613902Z     134.            # and then just extract them, because zipfile extraction prevents
2025-06-13T00:38:42.1614500Z     135.            # traversal unlike tarfile extraction
2025-06-13T00:38:42.1614964Z     136.            context.write_verbose(
2025-06-13T00:38:42.1615483Z     137.                f"unpack: {member.filename} -> {path/member.filename}"
2025-06-13T00:38:42.1615984Z     138.            )
2025-06-13T00:38:42.1616403Z     139.            unzipped.append(Path(zf.extract(member, path=path)))
2025-06-13T00:38:42.1616983Z     140.    return Path(os.path.commonpath(unzipped))

2025-06-13T00:40:36.4574374Z     31.It definitely will not work on any machine architecture that presents the docker container with an arm64 environment (e.g. M1/M2 macs, phones, embedded devices). The buildroot SDK that powers the package builds needs to run in an x86_64 architecture. If that's all you have, you'll have to run it in a cross-architecture vm.
2025-06-13T00:40:36.4575829Z     32.
2025-06-13T00:40:36.4576089Z     33.## Build Tools
2025-06-13T00:40:36.4576309Z     34.
2025-06-13T00:40:36.4576904Z     35.The build tools are a separate package that is developed in this repo in `./tools` - see that directory for more.
2025-06-13T00:40:36.4577476Z     36.
2025-06-13T00:40:36.4577755Z     37.## Adding a New Package
2025-06-13T00:40:36.4578004Z     38.
2025-06-13T00:40:36.4579488Z     39.To add a package to the repo, make a new set of directories in the `package/` directory for it. A best practice is to make that directory named `package_name/version` - for instance, `package/pandas/1.5.0`. For instance, if you wanted to add another version of pandas, you could add `package/pandas/2.0.0`.
2025-06-13T00:40:36.4581098Z     40.
2025-06-13T00:40:36.4582611Z     41.In that directory, make a python script called `build.py`.  Its job is to call `build.build_package` with correct arguments. A good example is `package/pandas/1.5.0/build.py`. `build.build_package` lives in `tools/builder/package_build/orchestrate.py` if you want to look at the source. 
2025-06-13T00:40:36.4584196Z     42.
2025-06-13T00:40:36.4585150Z     43.The `source` argument takes an object describing where to get the package. There is a helper function to build it: `builder.package_build.github_source`, which lets you specify
2025-06-13T00:40:36.4586323Z     44.- the github org (i.e. `pandas-dev` for pandas)
2025-06-13T00:40:36.4586844Z     45.- the github repo (i.e. `pandas` for pandas)
2025-06-13T00:40:36.4587307Z     46.- a git tag to find
2025-06-13T00:40:36.4588403Z     47.- some other options about how the package is distributed and what kind of source it is. Check out `tools/builder/package_build/__init__.py` to see more.
2025-06-13T00:40:36.4589410Z     48.
2025-06-13T00:40:36.4591764Z     49.Then, you set the `setup_commands` (typically these will be `build_ext` and `bdist_wheel`, but it depends on the package) and any build dependencies. Build dependencies are probably listed in the package metadata; they may be there as `setup_depends` or pyproject toml build system requirements. They may also just be assumed to be present. You can figure out what's required by reading the package code, or by trying to build it in an empty venv.
2025-06-13T00:40:36.4593723Z     50.

2025-06-13T00:45:33.3637652Z   +- **Version**: 3.3.4

2025-06-13T00:45:33.3638815Z   +
2025-06-13T00:45:33.3638983Z   +## Build Configuration
2025-06-13T00:45:33.3639174Z   +
2025-06-13T00:45:33.3639383Z   +The `build.py` file configures the build system to:
2025-06-13T00:45:33.3639648Z   +

2025-06-13T00:45:33.3640884Z   +
2025-06-13T00:45:33.3641063Z   +## Dependencies with Native Components
2025-06-13T00:45:33.3641455Z   +
2025-06-13T00:45:33.3641729Z   +Prefect has several runtime dependencies that include compiled components:
2025-06-13T00:45:33.3642073Z   +

2025-06-13T00:45:33.3642994Z   +- `cryptography` - Cryptographic library with native code
2025-06-13T00:45:33.3643516Z   +- `orjson` - Fast JSON library with Rust extensions  
2025-06-13T00:45:33.3644138Z   +- `pydantic-core` - Pydantic validation core with Rust extensions
2025-06-13T00:45:33.3644660Z   +- `ujson` - Fast JSON library with C extensions
2025-06-13T00:45:33.3645193Z   +- `websockets` - WebSocket library with C extensions
2025-06-13T00:45:33.3645639Z   +
2025-06-13T00:45:33.3646422Z   +These dependencies are handled automatically by the build system and will be resolved from the package index or built separately if needed.
2025-06-13T00:45:33.3647073Z   +
2025-06-13T00:45:33.3647427Z   +## Build Process
2025-06-13T00:45:33.3647805Z   +

2025-06-13T00:45:33.3651264Z   +```
2025-06-13T00:45:33.3651536Z   +
2025-06-13T00:45:33.3651812Z   +The build system will:
2025-06-13T00:45:33.3652226Z   +1. Pull the Prefect source code from GitHub
2025-06-13T00:45:33.3652816Z   +2. Set up a cross-compilation environment using Buildroot SDK
2025-06-13T00:45:33.3653491Z   +3. Create a Python virtual environment with build dependencies
2025-06-13T00:45:33.3654109Z   +4. Build the wheel package for arm7hf architecture
